# Feature Inventory

> Every user-visible capability of the product — each page, panel, tab, modal, action and settings
> toggle reachable from `frontend/src/App.tsx`, and each external interface — traced UI → API →
> backend route → service → store, with a status and the evidence for it. The owner rules from this
> map in the Phase 2 RULING session (`R2`, UR-13); after `R2` it records each feature's ruling and is
> kept current.

**Owner:** the frontend lane (`docs/uplevel/README.md`, "Lanes"). **Created by:** `F2.2`
(`docs/uplevel/history/packages-done.md`). **Traced at:** `main` `a890fd309`; `backend/` there is
byte-identical to the tree [`backend-entry-points.md`](backend-entry-points.md) traced, so its
cites hold here. Re-checked after merging `main` `eafc7148f`: of the files the rows cite, only
`backend/services/container_discovery.py` changed (`B1.6`), and F-140's two cites into it were
re-aimed. Re-checked again after merging `main` `9eb963ebf`: R47 (#6943) shifted every `backend/main.py` cite (re-aimed by diff) and wired the system broadcaster into the event emitter, which changed F-008's and F-016's evidence and §7's last row.

**Target Audiences:** the owner (R2), lane agents (Phase 3 deletions, Phase 4 feature packages),
reviewers.

---

## 1. How to read this document

### Statuses

The vocabulary is the README's (`docs/uplevel/README.md`, "Vocabulary"); no other status appears.

| status                 | meaning here                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **unverified**         | the chain exists end to end and the store it reads has a production writer (or the store it writes has a reader), but no golden path proves it yet. `F2.3` promotes it to **works** or demotes it to **half-built**. `R2b`'s rounds add a second way to sit here: the CI stack cannot reach the leg at all (owner ruling 127), and the evidence note names what `F2.3` will seed or run on the real tier to reach it.                  |
| **half-built**         | the chain breaks somewhere between the user and the store, and the evidence cites where by `path:line`: the route is missing or takes another verb (the client gets 404 or 405), the handler returns fixed or mock data, the store has no production writer, the handler reports success it did not achieve, a write has no reader — or the break is at the UI end: a control wired to nothing, or a built component no route renders. |
| **leftover**           | a surface for a capability that was retired (R8 and earlier: Florence-2, CLIP, X-CLIP, FashionCLIP, YOLO-World, pose and action recognition, the enrichment service, the Nemotron LLM, plate OCR); the evidence cites the retirement and what still shows it.                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **awaiting real tier** | golden path green on the fake stack; the real tier has not run. Ruling 103; the operator's `F2.3` real-tier run promotes it to **works**, and the evidence cites the green spec.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **works**              | the feature's golden path is green on the fake stack and confirmed on the real tier (README, "Vocabulary"). Ruling 148; the operator's real-tier confirmation sets it — a lane may not set it from its own green fake-tier run, which is `awaiting real tier`. The evidence cites the green spec and the real-tier run.                                                                                                                |

**A break at the UI end is half-built — a reading the owner is asked to confirm** (PR #6941, "Questions for the owner"; `R2` rules these rows either way). The README's examples of half-built are backend
faults, but the definition ("the surface exists, but … reports success it did not achieve") reads
the same from the other end. A button whose success toast covers a write that never happens, a
filter the list never applies, and a component built and wired to the API that no route renders are
each a feature the owner can **complete** or **retire** — which is what `R2` needs. Such a row says
"rendered by no route" in its surface, and its evidence cites the break.

### Row format (DECIDE)

One row per capability whose status could differ from its neighbours: a read-only page that works
is one row; an action that writes, or a panel whose data comes from a different chain, gets its own.

| column    | holds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| id        | `F-nnn`, stable: never renumbered, never reused. New rows take the next free number.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| surface   | what a user would call it, then its route(s) in backticks (`/settings/cameras`); global chrome and external interfaces carry no route                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| action    | what the user does and sees                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| API calls | each request as `` `METHOD /path` ``, `<br>`-separated; WebSockets as `` `WS /ws/…` ``. Parameter names are free: `{id}` and `{camera_id}` compare equal                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| backend   | route handler at `path:line` → service → store (table, Redis key, file, outbound call)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| status    | **unverified**, **half-built**, **leftover**, **awaiting real tier** or **works**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| evidence  | why that status, with `path:line` cites: the production writer for **unverified**, the break for **half-built**, the retirement for **leftover**, the green spec for **awaiting real tier**, the green spec and the real-tier run for **works**                                                                                                                                     |
| modules   | every module the feature uses, in every lane, as repo-relative paths. Phase 3 deletes a retired feature's modules from this list; Phase 4 consolidates a completed feature's tests from it.                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| real tier | the date the real tier last confirmed the row (`F2.3`); `—` until then                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ruling    | the owner's ruling from `R2`, which records it: **complete**, **retire**, **split** (part of the row is kept and part retires), **defer to roadmap R1**, **complete, consolidated** (several rows become one feature) or **not ruled** (`R2b` round 1 records the return of a row `F2.3` could not reach). Rows already ruled carry the ruling: inbound webhooks `complete (UR-12)` |
| priority  | the owner's order among the completes, from `R2`: `P1`–`P4`, or `—` for a row recorded outside that order (a retirement, a split, or a return no tier was set for). A tier may carry a scheduling note: `P1, fix now` is a bug fix that queues ahead of the Phase 4 order (owner ruling 135)                                                                                        |

The feature tables (§2 onward) are each preceded by `<!-- prettier-ignore -->`, so editing one row
never re-pads the others. The two vocabulary tables in this section are not, so a cell edit there
re-pads its own table — run Prettier rather than hand-padding.

### The gate

`frontend/src/__tests__/feature-inventory.test.ts` checks this file against the code on every CI
run:

- every route `App.tsx` declares appears in some row's surface;
- every request a file reachable from `src/main.tsx` can send — as F1.1's endpoint-contract scanner
  (`frontend/scripts/api-contract-lib.mjs`) reads them — appears in some row's API calls, or in §5;
- every production file `src/main.tsx` cannot reach is claimed by a row or listed in §4, and §4
  lists no reachable file;
- every row has a status from the vocabulary and evidence (a `path:line` cite for **half-built**);
  every cited file exists and every cited line is inside it; every module exists;
- `frontend/knip.json` enters only through `src/main.tsx` and checks exports;
- every entry in F1.1's known-missing list names the row that owns it (or §5).

So a PR that adds a page, a request or a file without a row fails CI. **The gate's reach is the
scanner's.** It cannot see a request whose URL is held in a variable before `fetch`, composed onto a
base by a helper (`fetchAuditApi`, `fetchPromptApi`, `fetchModelZooApi`), built with `new URL`, or
opened through `buildWebSocketOptions`; the tracing for this file read those call sites by hand and
lists their requests in the rows, but the gate does not hold them there.

To read every cite beside the line it points at today, run
`node frontend/scripts/feature-inventory-cites.mjs` from the repo root. Line numbers drift as code
changes; a row whose cite no longer says what the evidence claims is re-traced, not re-numbered.

### The witness

The `status` column is held to the golden run (ruling 218). After the golden project, both
Feature check jobs run `scripts/uplevel/inventory_witness.py`, which maps each golden test the run
executed to the rows whose `F-nnn` its title carries (the innermost describe or test title naming
any; `F-240 to F-243` and `F-164..F-167` name every id between; a pytest test spells it
`test_f100_…`). For a row at **awaiting real tier** or **works**, a mapped test that failed or was
skipped fails the job that ran it. A job also fails such a row when no test in its run names it
and the job is the row's home: the seeded job for a row that only `*.seeded.spec.ts` titles name,
the fake AI job for a row only other golden specs' titles name, both jobs when both do; a row no
title names takes its home from the specs its evidence cites. An **unverified** row whose tests
all passed is printed as ready to flip, with evidence text to paste. Legs the owner is ruling on
are excused by name, with why, in `scripts/uplevel/inventory_witness_known.toml`. So a golden
spec's title names the rows it witnesses, and an evidence cell names the spec and its legs. Run and
job ids are no longer required there, since the check re-proves the claim at every head the jobs
run; existing ones may stay.

Break cited per the Statuses section above (a UI-end break is half-built and the owner rules it complete-or-retire either way). Two measurement traps worth keeping: `isTablet` (`640-1023`) FORCE-collapses the rail whatever the user chose (`:204`, `isCollapsed = forceCollapsed ?? (isTablet \|\| (isDesktop && userCollapsed))`) and the toggle renders only at >=1024, so the desktop leg runs at 1440px; and the slide must be asserted as geometry (`boundingBox().x` off-canvas -> 0) because the class list carries `md:translate-x-0` UNCONDITIONALLY -- a `/translate-x-0/` regex passes while the drawer is still off-screen. Phone leg (375px via `test.use({ viewport })`; `loginAsAdmin` seeds a cookie so the boot is viewport-independent): bottom bar `role=navigation` "Mobile navigation", "Go to Timeline" navigates, the More sheet's `mobile-nav-item-entities` navigates AND unmounts the sheet (`frontend/src/components/common/BottomSheet.tsx:186-188` AnimatePresence), and the hamburger yields `mobile-overlay` visible with `sidebar` count 0 -- the defect -- then x->0 at 700px, where the clause does pass. Green standalone 13.3s and mid-suite (23 passed / 1 pre-existing skip, measured 2026-10-11); **works** waits on the real tier, ruling 66, and the slide-out half is ruled -- owner ruling 108 (`6109755853`, 2026-10-11): `half-built` stands, fix-or-retire at R2b. **[r130f] FIX + FLIP (2026-10-12, ruling 130 -- every [r130f] row's golden spec is retyped to assert the fix and must pass, which resolves ruling 108's open fix-or-retire toward FIX).** The two gates were made to agree by DELETING the JS one: `Layout.tsx` now mounts `<Sidebar />` unconditionally and the existing CSS is the single display contract. The direction was chosen from the dependency, not from a taste for a number: moving the hamburger to 640 would strand the feature it opens (`MobileBottomNav.tsx:58-63`'s More sheet is the phone's in-page nav, so a phone keeps needing the drawer), while the Sidebar doubles as the desktop RAIL and so cannot be unmounted at >=768 either -- which leaves "always mounted, CSS decides". The tell the original evidence had already named is what confirmed the direction: the drawer's own "Menu / Close" header is gated `md:hidden` ALONE (`Sidebar.tsx:305`), markup reachable below 768 only if the component mounts below 768. (Line-number note for the pre-fix citations above: they were taken against the file as it stood at demotion time, and this fix inserts lines, so `Sidebar.tsx:267` is now `:296`, `:271` is now `:301`, `:276-284` is now `:306-314`, and `Layout.tsx:93` no longer exists -- the mount is unconditional.) **A second, NEW defect that always-mounting creates, caught by measurement rather than assumption:** below `md` the drawer is hidden by `-translate-x-full`, and a transform moves an element visually WITHOUT removing it from the sequential focus order -- at 375px the CLOSED drawer exposed 34 tabbable links and put `close-menu-button` at Tab stop #5, a WCAG 2.4.3 break that did not exist while the component was unmounted. Fixed in the same commit with `inert={breakpoint === 'sm' && !isMobileMenuOpen}`, keyed on `breakpoint` (which is `'sm'` below `BREAKPOINTS.md` = 768, `useViewport.ts:57-62`) and NOT on `isMobile` (`< 640`, `:124`): at 700px `isMobile` is false while the drawer is still a drawer, so an `isMobile`-keyed gate would inert a drawer the user can see and has just opened. **Witness: `frontend/tests/golden/shell/nav.spec.ts` (spec blob `e11d19311dfe`), 2 passed (15.2s) EXECUTED on the F2.3 probe stack — WITNESS-INTEGRITY NOTE ([r130f] self-review, UR-128): the shipped blob at this head is `653010eec`, not the quoted `e11d19311dfe`. `a54ce354b` rewrote the file AFTER that probe run (+13/−1: an `(link as HTMLElement)` cast and an `if (!a) return 'NO-FOCUS'` null-guard, both verdict-neutral in intent), so those bytes never executed and citing them as this row's golden witness would be false. What the shipped blob DOES carry is execution evidence: the `Golden Spec Type Check (TypeScript)` job compiles it, and `Sidebar.test.tsx`'s 15 unit tests cover the same three mutations. The golden witness for the shipped bytes is the first `Feature check (fake AI)` run on a ready head, named at the flip.** (compose project `workspace`; UI served by this branch as a PRODUCTION build, API via the 127.0.0.1:18080 forwarder), phone leg 6.9s, desktop leg 7.2s. Serving a PRODUCTION build is load-bearing, not incidental: a `vite dev` UI mounts `<ReactQueryDevtools>` (`App.tsx:353`) and its floating logo intercepts the phone bottom bar's More-sheet click (`<ellipse> from <div class="tsqd-parent-container"> intercepts pointer events`), a pure local artifact that CI (nginx serving the built bundle) never sees. Three mutations, each red at its own assertion: pre-fix `Layout.tsx` (from `git show HEAD:`) -> `sidebar` count 0 vs 1 at the phone leg's first assert; `inert={false}` -> the `toHaveJSProperty('inert', true)` assert and the unit test both red; `isMobile && !isMobileMenuOpen` -> the unit tier's discriminating 700px test red. The phone leg now asserts BOTH halves of the row's mobile clause at ONE width (bottom bar navigates AND hamburger slides the drawer to `x === 0`), the closed drawer's inertness by three independent instruments (`inert` property, programmatic `focus()` refused, and a 10-stop Tab walk with no `SIDEBAR:` stop) mirrored by the OPEN drawer's reachability so none of them can pass on a drawer unreachable for an unrelated reason, and the backdrop close by a `position: {x: 340}` tap because the full-width overlay's CENTRE is covered by the open drawer (z-40 over z-30) -- a new property of always-mounting. Four unit tests cover the gate's three branches (`src/components/layout/Sidebar.test.tsx`, whose `useViewport`/`useSidebarContext` mocks had to become mutable -- with a constant `breakpoint: 'lg'` the tier is blind to this behaviour), 165 green across `src/components/layout/`. **Not covered, stated:** whether the closed drawer leaves the ACCESSIBILITY TREE (Chromium's `ariaSnapshot` enumerates all 80 lines inert or not, so no claim is made -- only focusability is measured), where focus lands when the drawer closes by button or backdrop (measured: `<body>`, so the next Tab resumes FORWARD from the drawer's DOM position rather than at the top -- the app's modals do the same, cf. the F-018 row), and the drawer has NO Escape-to-close at all. **Executed-honesty note on the legacy tier:** `frontend/tests/e2e/specs/mobile-optimization.spec.ts:82,:190` asserted `sidebar` `not.toBeVisible()`, which the fix falsifies TWICE over (Playwright calls a translated element visible -- it has a 256px box at x=-256 -- and "absent" was never the promise), and both were retyped to count 1 + `box.x < 0` + `inert` (blobs `91e3baead555`). They were NOT executed green: that whole file cannot boot in this sandbox -- it lands on the First Time Setup screen, and the same failure reproduces with my two source files STASHED, so it is a pre-existing baseline gap, not this diff. The file also self-skips on CI (`test.skip(() => !!process.env.CI)`), which is why it survived. **CI WITNESS LANDED (recorded by `[r130f]`'s register tick, 2026-10-14):** #7039 MERGED at 16:50:44Z (`01e02136d`, PR head `ec92ab3ec`), and the deferral just promised is discharged -- CI's `Feature check (fake AI)` job `115961340950` (run 38594610450) ran that head and PASSED both `shell/nav` legs, desktop and phone, on spec blob `653010eec`, which `git rev-parse ec92ab3ec:frontend/tests/golden/shell/nav.spec.ts` confirms is the shipped blob rather than the never-executed `e11d19311dfe`. The Test Performance Audit job still SKIPS a frontend-only diff by its own `if:` condition in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (re-read at `origin/main`, unchanged), so per ruling 128 this row's witness is that executed+passed run. Both openers are pinned: Control+k (`frontend/src/hooks/useKeyboardShortcuts.ts:122-126` -- the Meta leg is the same branch, `metaKey \|\| ctrlKey`, and Control is what a Linux runner can press portably) and the Search... button (`frontend/src/components/layout/Header.tsx:300-312`, `data-testid=search-trigger`, reaching state through `useCommandPaletteContext`, provider at `frontend/src/components/layout/Layout.tsx:77-85`; it is `hidden sm:flex`, so the trigger only EXISTS at >=640 and the spec runs at 1440). The fuzzy clause is pinned branch-by-branch off the filter at `frontend/src/components/common/CommandPalette.tsx:190-210` (combined = value + keywords, both lowercased; a WORD starting the query scores 1, a plain substring 0.5, else 0), with each query's branch unique by construction rather than by taste: `tim` matches Timeline by NAME word-start only; `hist` matches Timeline AND Audit Log ONLY through the joined keywords (no NAME contains `hist` -- if the keywords join ever breaks this leg fails loud instead of passing vacuously) and their tie keeps registration order; `ANALYT` pins case-insensitivity; `atus` reaches System on the substring arm alone (`status` CONTAINS it, no word starts with it) with everything else gone; `zzzznotathing` empties the list into `No results found.`. Select is measured both ways -- Enter on a filtered list, and ArrowDown-then-Enter on the full list, which also pins the footer's arrow-key hint and Dashboard-first registration -- and each hop asserts `not-found-page` count 0 so a jump to an UNREGISTERED path cannot masquerade as a pass; selecting also UNMOUNTS the dialog (`:167-169`). `g`-chords are pinned from `frontend/src/hooks/useKeyboardShortcuts.ts:19-29` and `:143-164`: `g t` -> `/timeline`, `g a` -> `/alerts`, `g d` -> `/`, and the 1000 ms `CHORD_TIMEOUT` branch itself is measured (arm `g`, wait 1.3 s, press `a` -- the URL must NOT move), because a spec that only tests the happy path cannot tell a working window from an immortal one; an unknown second key cancels without navigating. `?` opens the help modal (`frontend/src/components/common/ShortcutsHelpModal.tsx:112-119`) and its CONTENT is pinned, not just its visibility -- heading, all three groups, and two rows key-for-key -- because the row's promise is that table. **Two measurement traps, both cost a run and both are now in the spec's comments.** (1) An Escape pressed while `activeElement` is still `<body>` is a SILENT no-op: the palette's `onKeyDown` is a React delegated handler at the root container and a body-targeted keydown never bubbles UP to it, while `frontend/src/components/layout/Layout.tsx:60` disables the global hook whenever a modal is open -- and the component focuses its own input on `setTimeout(0)` (`:141-148`), which can land after the next test action, so EVERY reopen waits for `toBeFocused` before it trusts a keystroke. (2) `frontend/src/components/layout/Layout.tsx:60`'s `enabled: !isCommandPaletteOpen && !isShortcutsHelpOpen` is what makes Escape single-fire (each dialog owns its own handler: `frontend/src/components/common/CommandPalette.tsx:158-165`, `frontend/src/hooks/useFocusTrap.ts:173-176`) and is asserted from BOTH sides -- typing `?` or pressing Ctrl+k inside the palette input must not reach the hook (`isEditableElement` guard, `:56-71`, `:115-117`), and Ctrl+k while the HELP modal is open must not open the palette. Focus behaviour is measured rather than assumed: the trap focuses the Close button first (`:129-144`) and returns focus to `<body>` on close. Wire leg: booting a path this stack has never served batches its Web Vitals into exactly ONE `POST /api/rum` at ~10.1 s — the shipped `flushIntervalMs=10000` timer (`frontend/src/services/rum.ts:93-99`, `:132`), not the batch, since 4 metrics < `batchSize` 5 (`:182`) — and `ingest_rum_metrics` answers 200 with `errors: []` and `metrics_count` equal to the body's length (`backend/api/routes/rum.py:86-99`, `backend/api/schemas/rum.py:127-141`). Store leg: the same run lands as `hsi_rum_{lcp,fcp,ttfb,page_load_time}_seconds_count{path="<that path>"}` with `_sum > 0` on `/api/metrics` — the value survives `observe_rum_*`'s ms→s division (`backend/core/metrics.py:2602`) — and NO other family exists for that path, which is what makes the read attributable to this boot alone on a registry every past boot has written. Two findings from building it: (1) a boot with NO input reports only TTFB/FCP/PAGE_LOAD_TIME — web-vitals finalizes **LCP on first input**, proven by probing a bare boot (trio) against the same boot plus input (four), and by the live registry, where all 6 `hsi_rum_lcp_seconds` series belong to paths other specs click; the press must come after web-vitals' async import mounts (`frontend/src/services/rum.ts:372`) and must not navigate, or `reportMetric` re-stamps the metric on the destination path (`frontend/src/services/rum.ts:169-172`). (2) **INP and CLS are asserted nowhere: measured absent** (focus moved twice, `visibilityState` still `visible`, 22 s) — INP needs an input followed by a real presentational change and CLS a layout shift, neither of which the catch-all page produces; manufacturing either would be a vacuous expect (ruling 86), and closing a context is no substitute for a hide (`flushWithBeacon` never fired: no extra family landed after close, polled 25 s). **`works` waits on the real tier (ruling 66)**; the Grafana-read clause belongs to F-294. | `frontend/src/main.tsx`, `frontend/src/services/rum.ts`, `backend/api/routes/rum.py`, `backend/core/metrics.py` | — |  |  |

Measured from the failure message itself, which named rows 0/1 as `20:48:59.390743Z`/id 2 then `20:47:38.295147Z`/id 1 (correctly DESC), and confirmed by truth-tabling both forms against the shipped ORDER BY. Fixed by returning what a correct `started_at DESC, id DESC` comparator yields for the pair, so a correct page yields `>= 0`. No shipped claim was contradicted by this run; the row stays unverified until the repaired instrument has its own first-attempt green (ruling 114). Second run (Feature check (fake AI) run 38176486293 (head `0dbb08224`, 2026-10-11 21:28Z, read to completion from the run's own `golden-playwright.log` markers + retry directories, because junit collapses a retry-absorbed pass into one green)): the repaired sort guard PASSED, which is itself the proof the fix landed — the mirror leg now fails FURTHER DOWN, on all three attempts (✘ 3 / retry #1 4 / retry #2 5), on a new signature: `expect(locator).toHaveCount(expected) failed`, locator `getByTestId('detection-card-2').locator('[data-verdict]')`, expected 1, received 0. **This red is a shipped product finding, not the instrument.** The feed decides the chip from `event.verdict` (`frontend/src/components/dashboard/ActivityFeed.tsx:207-209`; the quote at `:186-188`), but the dashboard's own REST mapper rebuilds every event as a SIX-field literal — `id`, `camera_id`, `risk_score`, `risk_level`, `summary`, `started_at` (`frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx:206-221`) — which DROPS `verification`/`verdict` on the floor. So a scored event whose API row carries a non-confirmed verdict loses its chip, which is precisely the case the component's comment says 1.6 was written to fix ("a scored `rejected` event gets Rejected — the interim chip's score-keying hid exactly that case", `:201-204`). Every sibling surface passes the value explicitly (`frontend/src/components/events/TimeGroupedEvents.tsx:295`, `frontend/src/components/events/DeletedEventCard.tsx:219`, `frontend/src/components/dashboard/TopEventsCarousel.tsx:440`); only this glue omits it, and the hand-written `SecurityEventData` twin declares `verification` but never a flattened `verdict` (`frontend/src/types/websocket.ts:121-127`), so the WebSocket-sourced half of the merged list likely reads the same dead branch (flagged, NOT asserted — no driven live frame here). The spec asserts the API's truth and stays RED as failure-as-evidence (ruling 86) rather than mirroring the drop; the fix is one mapper line + the `verdict` pass-through, frontend's own. The row stays unverified, and its green now requires the FIX, not another run (ruling 114). What the same run's sibling DID go red on is a different mechanism, and it is not the hazard: that file's reload leg failed attempt 1 with the app's own error page in the aria tree — `heading "Error Loading Dashboard"` + `paragraph: "HTTP 429: Too Many Requests"` — i.e. the nginx `zone=api` shed class (`frontend/docker-entrypoint.sh:139` `limit_req zone=api burst=20 nodelay`) landing on a boot, never reaching the persistence assert. The draft-discard signature did NOT reproduce. The hazard text above stays in force (it is read from shipped code, not from a flake) and remains stated in F-040's evidence; **works** waits on the real tier (rulings 66, 103, 114). Update from the next run (Feature check (fake AI) run 38178633849, head `9e2923ba2`): the Reset leg was green on the first attempt, but the enable/reorder and Cancel legs were red on attempt 1 and retry-absorbed. The Cancel leg red at `frontend/tests/golden/dashboard/config-modal.spec.ts:262` with the DRAFT-DISCARD signature proper — the save reported success (modal closed) yet `getByTestId('pipeline-telemetry')` resolved to nothing: the hazard stated in F-040's evidence DID reproduce on this row's own code after run 38173376883's non-reproduction. The reorder leg red at `:175` (`expect.poll(rowIndex).toBe(0)` received 3): a moved row regressed to its seeded index mid-loop, CONSISTENT with the same re-seed mechanism (`frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardConfigModal.tsx:109-113` with `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardLayout.tsx:268`), but no deterministic trigger is nameable from shipped code, so it is recorded as a flake with a candidate mechanism, not an asserted cause. The flip earned on c2a5fdded's first-attempt greens stands (ruling 114 — a later run's flake does not unflip a witnessed row). Update (run 38181698242): all three legs green on the first attempt (✓ 10, ✓ 11, ✓ 12), no retry directory — the draft-discard hazard did NOT fire on this run either. The shipped reading of the hazard stands unchanged (it is code, not weather). **Update — the hazard is fixed, and its cause is now named rather than candidate** (this PR, fix commit `c997b0b7d`): the third clause above ("the layout persists across reloads") was the one this row withheld its flip for, and the mechanism it blamed is now closed at `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardConfigModal.tsx:108-123` — the draft seeds on the closed→open edge, so the `{ widgets, version }` literal that `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardLayout.tsx:268` mints per render can no longer overwrite unsaved toggles and reorders mid-edit. This corrects the sentence "no deterministic trigger is nameable from shipped code, so it is recorded as a flake with a candidate mechanism, not an asserted cause": the MECHANISM is nameable and was reproduced deterministically in jsdom on unmodified `origin/main` (open the modal, edit, hand it three equal-valued repaints — the uncommitted edit is gone), so the reorder leg's red at `frontend/tests/golden/dashboard/config-modal.spec.ts:176` (run 38450973581 attempt 1 — Received 5, 6 and 1 across its three Playwright attempts, three different wrong rows) is a CANDIDATE for that named cause, not a proven instance: the same workflow run's attempt 2, at the same head `07533cab1` with no fix in between, reports `tests=132 failures=0` (artifacts `11841961978` vs `11845772520`, each parsed) and `##[error] Process completed with exit code 1` appears only in attempt 1's log. A leg that reddens then greens on an unchanged head is weather by rulings 103/114's own standard, so the mechanism is proven in jsdom and its role in THIS run's red stays an inference. Stated at its true weight: the jsdom reproduction proves the DEFECT, not that a golden-tier repaint fired mid-loop in that run — the golden attribution remains an inference, but a named one anchored to a reproduced mechanism plus each run's own signature, where before the row had only a candidate. **No status change and no new witness are claimed here** (rulings 103, 114): the row stays **awaiting real tier**, its three legs keep the greens they earned, and the fix's own golden evidence is the next Feature check (fake AI) run's to produce. Unit-level: 2 new tests in `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardConfigModal.test.tsx` under "parent re-renders while open" — one asserts edits survive three equal-valued repaints, the other asserts a config changed while the modal was CLOSED still seeds fresh on open, so the edge-key has both halves covered. | `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardConfigModal.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/dashboard/DashboardLayout.tsx`, `frontend/src/stores/dashboard-config-store.ts` | 2026-10-13 |  |  |

`/ai`, `/ai-audit`, `/ai-services`, `/video-analytics`, `/settings/prompts`, `/settings/ai-models`, `/settings/calibration`.

| F-102 | AI Performance page › Grafana dashboard embed `/ai` | opens the page; sees the header, "Open in Grafana" link, Refresh button and the embedded `ai-services` Grafana dashboard (kiosk mode) | `GET /api/system/config` | `get_config` at `backend/api/routes/system.py:2474` → `Settings.grafana_url`; iframe → Grafana dashboard uid `ai-services` | unverified | `grafana_url` comes from settings in the config response (`backend/api/routes/system.py:2474`, a route that self-declares deprecation per the backend trace Dead Marks (f)); the embedded dashboard exists (`monitoring/grafana/dashboards/ai-services.json:4413` uid `ai-services`). **Break, found building the F2.3 golden batch `frontend/tests/golden/ai-pages/`:** the iframe this page embeds cannot load on the acceptance stack: `docker-compose.ci.yml` ships no grafana service (backend, frontend, postgres, redis + the fake-AI overlay's ai-vlm/ai-gateway), and the iframe's own src — measured 2026-10-11 through the CI frontend's nginx — `GET /grafana/d/ai-services/ai-services?orgId=1&kiosk=1&theme=dark&refresh=30s` answers 502 Bad Gateway. The page's Model Zoo half IS green (`frontend/tests/golden/ai-pages/model-zoo-section.spec.ts`, F-104); the dashboard embed this row names never renders there. **Why the row is `unverified` again (owner ruling 127):** the CI compose ships no Grafana service, so this is a reachability gap on the acceptance stack, not a product break — the chain exists end to end. **Route (ruling 144): its witness comes from the `O2.5` production-shape run** (the operator's run — `feature_check.py --real --prod-shape`, brought up on the prod-shape test deployment, not the per-batch CI lane). Why the per-batch lane cannot witness it, recorded while building ruling 127's per-batch tier: the harness rebuilds its deployment service-by-service from a hard keep-list picked by mode (`scripts/feature_check.py:425`: golden runs keep `scripts/feature_check.py:87`, real-tier runs keep `scripts/feature_check.py:107`, and neither list holds `grafana`), so a `grafana` service added to `docker-compose.ci.yml` is filtered back out; the other real-tier recipe pins its selection to `backend postgres redis vlm-proxy` (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/render-test-compose.sh:112`) and dies on any other service (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/render-test-compose.sh:115`) and already excludes `grafana` by name (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/docker-compose.b14-real.yml:201`). So only `O2.5`'s prod-shape bring-up (with its own override) can mount Grafana for this row; reaching it there is a harness change as well as a compose change, and that work belongs to `O2.5`'s owner on ops-a's roster (`docs/uplevel/packages/ops.md:153`) — the runner's own override is scoped today to the engine socket, the 7 pinned `container_name`s and the host ports/paths (`docs/uplevel/packages/ops.md:176`), so standing up an absent service is beyond what that override does. | F-106 | AI Audit page › Dashboard tab (quality score metrics, recommendations, period selector, refresh) `/ai-audit` | opens the page; picks a period (24h–90d) or clicks Refresh; sees quality/consistency/utilisation stats and prompt-improvement recommendations | `GET /api/ai-audit/stats`<br>`GET /api/ai-audit/recommendations` | `get_audit_stats` at `backend/api/routes/ai_audit.py:255`, `get_recommendations` at `backend/api/routes/ai_audit.py:347` → `backend/services/pipeline_quality_audit_service.py` `get_stats`/`get_recommendations` → table `event_audits` | half-built | `event_audits` rows are written only by the batch audit job (`backend/api/routes/ai_audit.py:443` `create_partial_audit`, then `run_full_evaluation`); the pipeline creates none, so the page shows its empty state until a batch audit runs. **Break, found building the F2.3 golden batch `frontend/tests/golden/ai-pages/`:** every leg of this dashboard dies upstream of the page. The batch audit job fails 100% of events — measured job `a6a21dbe-6f1d-4c66-9af5-dcc01b03d88c` on the harness stack: `total_events: 3, processed_events: 0, failed_events: 3`, backend log the same second reads "Error processing event 152 in batch audit" + `sqlalchemy.exc.MissingGreenlet`: the job reads the DEFERRED column `Event.llm_prompt` (`backend/models/event.py:74`) at `backend/api/routes/ai_audit.py:445` inside a FastAPI background task, where the async session cannot lazy-load (the module's own `run_full_evaluation` docstring warns about exactly this and names the safe call). The job is the SOLE producer of `EventAudit` rows, and `get_stats` derives BOTH `total_events` and `audited_events` from audit rows (`backend/services/pipeline_quality_audit_service.py:658`), so stats answer `total_events: 0, audited_events: 0, message: "No events found for the selected period."` for days=7 AND days=90 with 3 events on the stack. `AIAuditPage` gates the ENTIRE tab group — the Dashboard tab, period selector and refresh this row names included — behind `hasData = stats.audited_events > 0` (`frontend/src/components/ai/AIAuditPage.tsx:195,:349`), so the page renders only its "No Events Have Been Audited Yet" block (:332). The tracking, re-ID, zone and loitering panels this row names never render there. **Why the row is `unverified` again (owner ruling 127):** the CI compose ships no Grafana service, so this is a reachability gap on the acceptance stack, not a product break — the chain exists end to end. **Route (ruling 144): its witness comes from the `O2.5` production-shape run** (the operator's run — `feature_check.py --real --prod-shape`, brought up on the prod-shape test deployment, not the per-batch CI lane). Why the per-batch lane cannot witness it, recorded while building ruling 127's per-batch tier: the harness rebuilds its deployment service-by-service from a hard keep-list picked by mode (`scripts/feature_check.py:425`: golden runs keep `scripts/feature_check.py:87`, real-tier runs keep `scripts/feature_check.py:107`, and neither list holds `grafana`), so a `grafana` service added to `docker-compose.ci.yml` is filtered back out; the other real-tier recipe pins its selection to `backend postgres redis vlm-proxy` (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/render-test-compose.sh:112`) and dies on any other service (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/render-test-compose.sh:115`) and already excludes `grafana` by name (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/docker-compose.b14-real.yml:201`). So only `O2.5`'s prod-shape bring-up (with its own override) can mount Grafana for this row; reaching it there is a harness change as well as a compose change, and that work belongs to `O2.5`'s owner on ops-a's roster (`docs/uplevel/packages/ops.md:153`) — the runner's own override is scoped today to the engine socket, the 7 pinned `container_name`s and the host ports/paths (`docs/uplevel/packages/ops.md:176`), so standing up an absent service is beyond what that override does. Measured in a browser on the harness stack: `pageerror` from the `AIModelsTab` chunk — "Cannot destructure property 'icon' of 't[s]' as it is undefined" — and `ai-models-tab` absent 13 s after navigation (app "Application Error" panel). Root cause: `ModelManagementPanel`'s `StatusIndicator` destructured `config[status]` over a closed 5-key map, but the endpoint behind its feed ships NO `status` field: `list_models` constructs `ModelStatus(name, category, estimated_vram_mb, enabled, service, gpu_id, runtime)` with no status kwarg (`backend/api/routes/model_management.py:631-639`); measured payload keys `[category, enabled, estimated_vram_mb, gpu_id, name, runtime, service]`. Same contract-drift family as OP-1's F-158, but fatal. **Fixed in [r130f]:** the panel no longer reads the shadowed shape. It derives the indicator state through `modelDisplayStatus` from the two fields the wire does send (`enabled` = registry config, `runtime.loaded` = live GPU state), reads VRAM from `GET /api/system/models/vram-summary`'s `totals`, and the `config[status]` lookup carries a `?? FALLBACK`, so an unheard-of value renders a neutral pill instead of blanking a settings tab. The spec asserts the payload's item key set `[category, enabled, estimated_vram_mb, gpu_id, name, runtime, service]` and `'status' in m === false` against the live body, then recomputes every figure the panel prints from that same body. **works** waits on the real tier. | `frontend/src/components/settings/AIModelsTab.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/settings/ModelZooPanel.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/settings/ModelCard.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/useModelZoo.ts`, `frontend/src/services/modelZooApi.ts`, `backend/api/routes/model_management.py`, `backend/api/schemas/model_management.py`, `backend/services/model_zoo.py` | — | complete — give the model status indicator a fallback for the `status` field the API does not send (ruling 130) | P1, fix now |

| F-158 | Operations › Developer tools › Configuration inspector `/operations` | expands Configuration Inspector; sees every setting (secrets redacted); copies one value or all as JSON | `GET /api/debug/config` | `get_config` at `backend/api/routes/debug.py:514` → iterate `get_settings()` with redaction | awaiting real tier |  Reads live settings in-process. **Break (found building the F2.3 golden batch `frontend/tests/golden/operations/`), CLOSED [r130f] and green on the real stack:** the inspector used to render the HTTP envelope, not the settings — `toConfigEntries` mapped `Object.entries` of the whole body, so the table showed 2 rows (`config`, `timestamp`) instead of the 303 settings measured under `config`, and redaction could never fire because `isSensitiveValue` matches the exact value `"[REDACTED]"` while the real redactions sit one level deeper. Fix: entries derive from `body.config` and `DebugConfigResponse` is read off the generated `components` map (`frontend/src/hooks/useDebugConfigQuery.ts:105`, `frontend/src/services/api.ts:7434`), so the type can no longer be a hand-written `Record<string, unknown>` mirror that accepts every shape — the unit tier stayed green through this bug precisely because of that mirror. **Second victim of the same envelope bug, found here and fixed with it:** `LoggingSettings` read its seven values off the envelope too and displayed the `'INFO'` default while the stack answered `config.log_level = 'WARNING'` (`frontend/src/components/settings/LoggingSettings.tsx:120-123`); its unit fixtures had been built from the same false flat shape, and 29 of its 32 tests passed either way because the fixture values coincide with the code's `??` defaults. Witness: `frontend/tests/golden/operations/config-inspector.spec.ts` (new in [r130f]) GREEN executed on the probe stack, run `probe-20261012231452`, `1 passed (4.0s)`, spec blob `e164ac9a8c28` (corrected [r130f] self-review: the previously cited `5d244c5fa6d5` is not an object in this repository at all — `git cat-file -t` fails — a transcription slip. The spec has had exactly ONE blob since it was created by `2099deab6` and is new in this PR, and it is this one, so the probe run's bytes and the shipped bytes are the same file); red against the pre-fix hook at the same head — `✘ 1 … (34.3s)`, `value-database_url` never appears because pre-fix the table has only the two envelope rows — so the spec discriminates. Its node leg asserts the envelope shape and recovers the redacted key and `log_level` from the same run, and its browser leg asserts row count == settings count, `value-config`/`value-timestamp` count 0, and that `database_url`'s password-only mask (`backend/api/routes/debug.py:350`) stays READABLE and unflagged. **Not yet covered:** the panel's Copy-All serializes the envelope `data` (its bytes are unchanged by this fix); and `SystemMonitoringPage.tsx:738` passes `data-testid="config-inspector-panel-section"` into a component that accepts `_props` (`frontend/src/components/developer-tools/ConfigInspectorPanel.tsx:73`) and never spreads it, so that testid is dropped — the spec pins the absence (count 0) so the prop-spread fix trips it. | `frontend/src/components/developer-tools/ConfigInspectorPanel.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/useDebugConfigQuery.ts`, `frontend/src/components/settings/LoggingSettings.tsx`, `frontend/src/services/api.ts`, `backend/api/routes/debug.py` | — | complete — map `body.config` rather than the envelope, so the settings show and the redaction fires (ruling 130) | P1, fix now |

| F-164 | Operations dashboard `/operations-dashboard` | opens the page; sees the embedded Grafana "HSI operations" dashboard; clicks Refresh or Open in Grafana | `GET /api/system/config` | `get_config` at `backend/api/routes/system.py:2474` → `grafana_url`; iframe → nginx `/grafana/` proxy → Grafana dashboard uid `hsi-consolidated` (`monitoring/grafana/dashboards/consolidated.json:15493`) | unverified | The dashboard uid the page embeds is provisioned (`monitoring/grafana/dashboards/consolidated.json:15493`); Grafana allows embedding (`docker-compose.prod.yml`). **Break, found building the F2.3 golden batch `frontend/tests/golden/operations/`:** the iframe this page embeds cannot load on the acceptance stack: `docker-compose.ci.yml` ships only backend, frontend, postgres and redis (the fake-AI overlay adds ai-vlm and ai-gateway) — grafana exists only in `docker-compose.prod.yml` — and measured through the CI frontend's own nginx proxy, `GET /grafana/api/health` answers 502 Bad Gateway. The embedded `hsi-consolidated` dashboard never renders on the stack the golden paths run against. **Why the row is `unverified` again (owner ruling 127):** the CI compose ships no Grafana service, so this is a reachability gap on the acceptance stack, not a product break — the chain exists end to end. **Route (ruling 144): its witness comes from the `O2.5` production-shape run** (the operator's run — `feature_check.py --real --prod-shape`, brought up on the prod-shape test deployment, not the per-batch CI lane). Why the per-batch lane cannot witness it, recorded while building ruling 127's per-batch tier: the harness rebuilds its deployment service-by-service from a hard keep-list picked by mode (`scripts/feature_check.py:425`: golden runs keep `scripts/feature_check.py:87`, real-tier runs keep `scripts/feature_check.py:107`, and neither list holds `grafana`), so a `grafana` service added to `docker-compose.ci.yml` is filtered back out; the other real-tier recipe pins its selection to `backend postgres redis vlm-proxy` (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/render-test-compose.sh:112`) and dies on any other service (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/render-test-compose.sh:115`) and already excludes `grafana` by name (`scripts/uplevel-real-tier/docker-compose.b14-real.yml:201`). So only `O2.5`'s prod-shape bring-up (with its own override) can mount Grafana for this row; reaching it there is a harness change as well as a compose change, and that work belongs to `O2.5`'s owner on ops-a's roster (`docs/uplevel/packages/ops.md:153`) — the runner's own override is scoped today to the engine socket, the 7 pinned `container_name`s and the host ports/paths (`docs/uplevel/packages/ops.md:176`), so standing up an absent service is beyond what that override does. An instrument that cannot run here is stated, not faked or left red (ruling 86). | `frontend/src/components/operations/OperationsDashboardPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/utils/grafanaUrl.ts`, `frontend/src/services/api.ts`, `backend/api/routes/system.py` | — | complete, consolidated — one Monitoring page with a dashboard picker over the provisioned dashboards, on one shared embed component (ruling 128) | P3 |

### 3.9 GPU, storage and admin settings

`/settings/gpu`, `/settings/storage`, `/settings/admin`.

| id | surface | action | API calls | backend | status | evidence | modules | real tier | ruling | priority |

| F-176 | GPU settings page › Detected GPUs `/settings/gpu` | opens Settings › GPU; sees one card per detected GPU with VRAM and the services assigned to it; clicks Refresh | `GET /api/system/gpus`<br>`GET /api/system/gpu-config` | `list_gpus` at `backend/api/routes/gpu_config.py:628` → `GpuDetectionService.detect_gpus` (pynvml / nvidia-smi); `get_gpu_config` at `backend/api/routes/gpu_config.py:658` → tables `system_settings`, `gpu_configurations` | unverified | GPU list is a live hardware probe on every request (`backend/api/routes/gpu_config.py:628`). **Break, found building the F2.3 golden batch `frontend/tests/golden/gpu-storage-admin/`**: the CI compose ships a GPU-less backend, so the live probe answers `{"gpus":[]}` and `frontend/src/pages/GpuSettingsPage.tsx:344` returns the EmptyState branch — the per-GPU VRAM cards and Refresh live in the has-GPU render (`frontend/src/pages/GpuSettingsPage.tsx:375`) that cannot mount here. Partial spec `frontend/tests/golden/gpu-storage-admin/gpu-page-no-hardware.spec.ts` pins the empty leg and asserts the branch controls `toHaveCount(0)`. **Why the row is `unverified` again (owner ruling 127):** the acceptance stack has no GPU for the backend to find, and this is a harness gap rather than only a compose gap: the harness never requests a GPU device for the backend, and `--real` "serves models only through `agent-gpu` (owner ruling 66); there is no host-GPU mode" (`scripts/feature_check.py:940-941`) — adding a service to `docker-compose.ci.yml` alone cannot hand the backend a card. **Route (ruling 144): its witness comes from the `O2.5` production-shape run** (the operator's run, which has `agent-gpu` per ruling 162 ¶1; a run without cgroup limits still witnesses ruling 127's 12 rows per ruling 162 ¶2). The break facts ruling 127 recorded stand: the fork its passthrough clause opened was answered owner-tier at build time (the option-2 consequence: "the rows' real tier shouldn't be waited on from the per-batch lane", `6127167216`, adopted by ops-b in `6127551397` §5) — on the reporter's own tier the device grant fails before compose (`docker run --device nvidia.com/gpu=0` exits 125, unresolvable CDI device — run-tier measurement `6127167216`, `6127700892`), so a keep-list patch plus a device stanza yields a stack that does not boot. Whether `O2.5`'s prod-shape run hands the backend a working card is a contingency the run must witness, not a fact ruling 162 establishes: `agent-gpu` (ruling 162 ¶1) is the VLM-serving path, not a host-GPU mode for the backend (`scripts/feature_check.py:940-941`), and the backend's own nvidia grant lives at `docker-compose.prod.yml:690` (`deploy.resources.reservations.devices`), which only a prod-shape bring-up pulls in — so resolution to a real card on the operator's runner is exactly what the `O2.5` run has to prove; neither per-batch cell closes alone (keep-list half cell B, device grant cell A, `6127551397` §5). Its compare leg is a separate product break that survives the real tier — `/gpu-config/history/diff` shadowed by `/gpu-config/history/{version_id}` at `backend/api/routes/gpu_config.py:1501` — so round 1 records it and raises it for the owner rather than rule it. | `frontend/src/components/settings/GpuVersionHistory.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/useGpuConfig.ts`, `frontend/src/services/gpuConfigApi.ts`, `backend/api/routes/gpu_config.py`, `backend/models/gpu_config.py` | — | not ruled — the CI stack cannot reach this leg, so round 1 does not rule it; `F2.3` reaches it (ruling 127) | — |

That commit is GitHub's ephemeral merge ref for this PR: `job.log:114` binds the sha to the ref in one line (`* [new ref] 31b962dc4761… -> pull/7181/merge`), and `job.log:160` prints git's own checkout subject `HEAD is now at 31b962d Merge adc9d3b48… into d4f8c0693…`, and "Merge X into Y" names both parents AND their order (parent1 `d4f8c0693` = main, parent2 `adc9d3b48` = head); the same SHAs are echoed in that run's build steps (`base.sha` `d4f8c0693…` at `job.log:1124`, `head.sha` `adc9d3b48…` at `:1281`, `merge_commit_sha` `31b962dc4…` at `:1322`). I did NOT read the parents from the commit object — `31b962dc4` is `refs/pull/7181/merge`, belongs to no branch, and `git cat-file` cannot read it from a normal clone (`summary.json` has no parents field either). The spec blob is byte-identical at that commit two independent ways: I replayed the merge (`git merge-tree --write-tree d4f8c0693 adc9d3b48` → tree `c69bdf93b`, whose `permission.spec.ts` blob is `5d76ba2d6`, 13,350 B), and GitHub's contents API at `ref=31b962dc4` returns that same sha `5d76ba2d6` / 13,350 B. Its results map (`job.log:2827`, parsed with `ast.literal_eval`) holds 214 Playwright entries under `playwright_specs` = 204 `passed` / 10 `skipped` / 0 failed, plus 19 `passed` under `backend_specs` — **223 `passed` total, and the unit is a results-map ENTRY (a spec + title key), not a test file**. The union of both dicts is 233 verdicts (223 `passed` + 10 `skipped`); a regex `'<key>': '(passed\|skipped)'` over the raw repr instead finds only 230 — it loses exactly 3 `passed`, one per ASCII apostrophe in a test title (`F-034 … is F-035's adjudicated row`, `the row's exact cache contract`, `the app's own backend POSTs`). An earlier draft of this cell reported "220" and attributed the gap to 204 − 3, which is 201, not 220: the correct reconciliation is 223 − 3 = 220, and that regex reading is superseded here by `literal_eval`; the 10 skips sit in 8 other spec files, so none of the three F-234 specs is a silent skip. Its junit says `tests="214" failures="0" errors="0" skipped="10"`, but that is no second opinion: `scripts/feature_check.py:2379` NAMES that file (`junit = self.artifacts / "golden-playwright.xml"`), `junit_results` (`:1188`, reading at `:1196`) parses it, and `:2539` builds the results map (`results["playwright_specs"] = junit_results(junit)`) — so map and junit are one measurement read twice. The independent witness is the `list` reporter's stdout `golden-playwright.log`, whose footer prints `10 skipped` then `204 passed (27.6m)` on two lines (`:220`-`:221`). No retry absorbed a failure, witnessed only by emitters that CAN print one: that log has 0 lines containing `retry` (that means `golden-playwright.log`, the list reporter's stdout — NOT `job.log`, where the 3 hits are backend pytest NAMES, not reporter markers: `test_retry_of_an_unknown_delivery_is_404` and `test_retry_resends_the_same_row_and_counts_the_attempt`, one line each, plus the results-map line that repeats both), 0 containing `flaky`, 0 `✘` results and 204 `✓` against its own `Running 214 tests` header, its 214 numbered slots are 1–214 with no gap or repeat (skips are dash-numbered inside the range — the clause bites via the anchor to the declared 214, not via the gap test alone: a retried run is still gapless, it just prints more slots than the run declared), and the junit carries 0 `ATTACHMENT` markers (the `[[ATTACHMENT` … `]]` form carries a pipe, so I count the token: 0 occurrences of the string `ATTACHMENT` in that file — and this is the only MARKER-BASED junit witness for the absorbed class, and one of TWO junit witnesses total: the flag-off path calls `_appendStdIO(entry, test.results)` (`:4595`, walking EVERY attempt at `:4619`) while the `failures`/`errors` tallies see only `test.ok()`. The second is `@time`, which on that SAME flag-off path (`:4594`, one line above my cite) is `test.results.reduce((a, v) => a + v.duration, 0) / 1e3` — the SUM of every attempt (controlled arm, identical 500 ms bodies: clean pass `time="0.506"`, absorbed `time="1.01"`). It is NOT independent — `list` already prints both attempts, so `time > any single attempt` is a derived read of the stdout witness — and it changes meaning with the flag nobody sets here: `:4573` (flag ON, absorbed) keeps only the LAST attempt's duration, `:4584` (flag ON, end-failed) only the FIRST. A third tell costs nothing: the marker PATHS name `-retry1` / `-retry2` output dirs, so an absorbed attempt is visible in junit path text with the flag off (my absorbed leg: `arms-absorbed-assertion` + `arms-absorbed-assertion-retry1`). The COUNT is a property of the rig's `use:` block, not of the reporter, so no per-attempt number here is portable (decomposition later in this cell): measured today with THIS repo's three settings (`frontend/playwright.config.ts:147-153`) and a `{ page }` fixture, an absorbed leg writes 4 and an always-fail leg 7 at `retries: 1` — but only 3 at `retries: 0`, because the `on-first-retry` trace needs a retry to exist at all. What survives every rig: `ATTACHMENT = 0` still earns "no attempt of any leg failed", because 0 sits below the floor of every arm). []` (`frontend/src/hooks/useFaceRecognitionApi.ts:150`), so `knownPersons?.length ?? 0` is no longer `undefined ?? 0` over a non-empty list, and the component-level mock that hid it is discharged by that PR's test updates. ## 4. Modules serving no feature

The modules no row claims. `R2` approves this list for deletion as a whole, naming any exceptions
(`docs/uplevel/templates/r2-sheet.md`, §3); `B3.2` and `F3.2` delete what it approves. **Ruled
(ruling 210):** approved as a rule, not as the snapshot below — each deletion package deletes what
its own check reports non-shipping at its head, outside `scripts/reachability/keep.toml`; `O3.1`
declares the `ai/` entry points first, so `ai/gateway/export/` stays; `synthbench/host`,
`generate` and `contract` stay (UR-82), and `synthbench/spikes/` goes.

### Frontend

The 136 production files under `frontend/src/` that `src/main.tsx` cannot reach (37583 lines), minus the
files a row claims. The same set is `npx knip --production --include files` (run in `frontend/`)
with this PR's `knip.json`, and the gate's own import walk (`reachableFiles` in
`frontend/scripts/feature-inventory-lib.mjs`); the two agree file for file. Tests that test only
these files go with them (`F3.2`). **Last meaningful commit** is the newest commit touching the file
that changed fewer than 100 files, so squash merges and lockfile sweeps do not mask a file's age.
`00-audit.md` §4.3 counted 236 unreachable files at `d6ba78d5` by a method it does not record [A];
this list is measured at this head by the two methods above, which agree, and is the one to rule on.

## 5. Client requests no feature reaches

Requests in files `src/main.tsx` does reach, made only by code nothing mounts. Two kinds, each named
in **why**:

- an exported function no production module imports — knip's unused exports
  (`npx knip --production` in `frontend/`);
- a function whose every reference chain stops short of a route — checked with
  `node scripts/feature-inventory-callers.mjs <file> <function>` in `frontend/` (TypeScript
  `findReferences`, production files only) — or one a mounted hook builds but whose consumer never
  takes it (`startService`, `createHousehold`, `deleteHousehold`, read at the consumer's line).

They are dead code in live files, not features; `F3.2` removes them with the rest of the unreachable
code. F1.1's known-missing entry for `GET /api/prompts/history/{model}` points here: the function
that sends it, `fetchModelHistory`, is one of them.

The `line N` in a why cell cites a line **inside** the function named in the row, and
`src/__tests__/feature-inventory.test.ts` checks that it still does, by asking the TypeScript
parser which top-level declaration encloses the number. A change that grows one of these files
moves every declaration below it, so a row can go stale without the change touching this file;
that is how `deleteKnownPerson` came to cite a line inside `updateKnownPerson`, when `a51a3aad9`
grew `useFaceRecognitionApi.ts` by 13 lines under a row this document had already written.

## 6. The audit's seeds, re-verified

`00-audit.md` §10 classified eleven surfaces before this inventory existed. Each was traced again;
where the answer changed, the row's evidence says why.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
| seed (`00-audit.md` §10) | seed status | rows | status now | what changed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Model Zoo status card | works, partly | F-104, F-129 (cards); F-105, F-130 (latency chart) | unverified; half-built | split: the cards read the live model manager; the latency chart has no writer (`record_model_zoo_latency` has no caller) |
| Model load/unload panel (`ModelZooPanel`) | works | F-125 (read side), F-128 (load/unload) | unverified; half-built | load and unload always answer 501 (Triton runs `--model-control-mode=none`) |
| `/reid` dashboard and Re-ID matches panel | half-built | F-085, F-086, F-065 | half-built | confirmed; the `entities` table has no production writer since R8 S2b removed the enrichment pipeline |
| Prompt management page | half-built | F-120, F-122, F-123, F-124; F-121 | half-built; leftover | the four retired-model forms are split out as **leftover** |
| Automatic AI-audit evaluation (`BackgroundEvaluator`) | half-built | F-114 | half-built | confirmed |
| Camera anomaly timeline | half-built | F-210 | half-built | the cause changed: the route is now served (`F1.1`), but `get_recent_anomalies` returns `[]` unconditionally |
| Inbound webhooks | half-built, ruled complete | F-290 | half-built | confirmed; they now answer 501 (`B1.3`) |
| MQTT commands | half-built | F-291 | half-built | confirmed; `mqtt_command_handler.py` now has no importer at all (the seed named `inbound_webhooks.py`) |
| Action events panel | leftover | F-068 | leftover | confirmed |
| Pose skeleton overlay | leftover | F-069 | leftover | confirmed |
| Alert conditions: pose, action, threat, smoke/fire | leftover | F-220 | leftover | confirmed; the UI hard-codes threat and smoke/fire off and omits pose/action |

## 7. Breaks that span rows

Several half-built rows share one cause: a store the UI reads that nothing in production writes, or
a producer nothing calls. Fixing or retiring the cause settles every row it lists, which is how `R2`
can rule them.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
| cause | evidence | rows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alert rules are never evaluated: `AlertRuleEngine.evaluate_event` and `create_alerts_for_event` have no production caller (the only construction is the test-rule dependency) | `backend/services/alert_engine.py:166`, `:1039`; `backend/api/dependencies.py:918` | F-216, F-217 |
| `entities` has no production writer since the enrichment pipeline was removed (R8 S2b) | `backend/services/entity_clustering_service.py:265`, `backend/services/hybrid_entity_storage.py:233` | F-065, F-081, F-082, F-083, F-085, F-086 |
| `scene_changes` has no production writer | `backend/services/scene_change_detector.py:307` | F-008, F-030, F-208, F-287, F-288 |
| `dwell_time_records` and the zone counters have no production writer | `backend/services/dwell_time_service.py:95`, `backend/services/line_zone_service.py:212`, `backend/services/polygon_zone_service.py:273` | F-260 to F-265, F-267 |
| `face_detection_events` has no production writer | `backend/services/face_recognition_service.py:588` | F-276, F-279, F-282, F-285 |
| `tracks` has no production writer | `backend/services/track_service.py:78` | F-289 |
| The heatmap accumulator is never fed | `backend/services/heatmap_service.py:197` | F-286 |
| WebSocket messages the UI waits for that nothing in production sends: several types have no producer at all, and the emitter's event broadcaster is never attached (R47, #6943, attached only the system one) | `backend/services/websocket_emitter.py:448`, `backend/main.py:1041`; the backend trace's Dead Marks (c) | F-008, F-030, F-091, F-136, F-144 |

## 8. Keeping this current

- **Adding a page, a request or a production file** fails the gate until a row (or §4/§5) accounts
  for it. Add the row in the same PR, with the next free id.
- **Changing a feature's chain** (a route moves, a writer appears or goes) updates its row's backend,
  evidence and modules in the same PR, under the README's cross-lane rule.
- **`F2.3`** sets **works** and the real-tier date per row, or demotes a row with its failing golden
  path as evidence; **`R2`'s record PR** fills ruling and priority (`R2a`'s recorded 2026-10-10 in #6980,
  ruling 89; `R2b` runs in rounds — round 1 recorded 2026-10-12 in #7030, rulings 127–135, ruling 86).
  A round records rulings without setting `R2b` to `done`: that happens when the last round is recorded.
- **Phase 3** deletes a retired row's modules and then the row; the id is not reused.
- `scripts/feature-inventory-cites.mjs` prints each cite beside its current line;
  `scripts/feature-inventory-callers.mjs` prints the chain from a client function up to the route that
  mounts it, or says nothing mounts it (both in `frontend/`).
